\displaystyle|P^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\triangle Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)|\leq\nu\binom{n}{j}.

Then
\mathscr{Q}
is a
(1/a_{1},\varepsilon+\nu^{1/6},\mathbf{a},\lambda+\nu^{1/6})
-equitable family of partitions which is an
(\varepsilon+\nu^{1/6},d_{\mathbf{a},k})
-partition of
G^{(k)}
.

The following lemma
shows that for every equitable family of partitions
\mathscr{P}
whose vertex partition
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
is an almost equipartition,
there is an equitable family of partitions with almost the same parameters whose vertex partition is an equipartition.

Lemma 5.12
.

Suppose
0<1/n\ll\lambda\ll\varepsilon\leq 1
,
k\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}
, and
R=(\varepsilon/3,\mathbf{a},d_{\mathbf{a},k})
is a regularity instance.
Suppose
\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}(k-1,\mathbf{a})
is a
(1/a_{1},\varepsilon,\mathbf{a},\lambda)
-equitable family of partitions and an
(\varepsilon,d_{\mathbf{a},k})
-partition of an
n
-vertex
k
-graph
H^{(k)}
.
Then there exists a family of partitions
\mathscr{Q}=\mathscr{Q}(k-1,\mathbf{a})
which is an
(\varepsilon+\lambda^{1/10},\mathbf{a},d_{\mathbf{a},k})
-equitable partition of
H^{(k)}
.

Proof.

Let
m:=\lfloor n/a_{1}\rfloor
.
We write
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}=\{V_{1},\dots,V_{a_{1}}\}
.
Since
\mathscr{P}
is a
(1/a_{1},\varepsilon,\mathbf{a},\lambda)
-equitable family of partitions,
we have
|V_{i}|=(1\pm\lambda)m
for all
i\in[a_{1}]
, and Lemma
5.5
implies that for each
j\in[k-1]
,
the function
\hat{P}^{(j)}(\cdot):\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a})\to\hat{\mathscr{P}}^{(j)}
is a bijection and
for each
j\in[k-1]\setminus\{1\}
, the function

P^{(j)}(\cdot,\cdot):\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a})\times[a_{j}]\to\mathscr{P}^{(j)}
is also a bijection.
Next, we fix the size of the parts in the new equitable partition
\mathscr{Q}
of
V:=V_{1}\cup\dots\cup V_{a_{1}}
.
For each
i\in[a_{1}]
, let
m_{i}:=\lfloor(n+i-1)/a_{1}\rfloor
.
Thus
m_{i}\in\{m,m+1\}
.
Choose
U^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq V_{i}
of size
\max\{|V_{i}|,m_{i}\}
and let
U^{\prime}_{0}:=\bigcup_{i\in[a_{1}]}V_{i}\setminus U^{\prime}_{i}
.
We partition
U^{\prime}_{0}
into
U^{\prime\prime}_{1},\dots,U^{\prime\prime}_{a_{1}}
in an arbitrary manner such that
|U^{\prime\prime}_{i}|=m_{i}-|U^{\prime}_{i}|
.
For each
i\in[a_{1}]
, let

\displaystyle U_{i}:=U^{\prime}_{i}\cup U^{\prime\prime}_{i}\text{ and }\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}:=\{U_{1},\dots,U_{a_{1}}\}.

Moreover, let
Q^{(1)}(b,b):=U_{b}
for each
b\in[a_{1}]
,
and
Q^{(1)}(b,b^{\prime}):=\emptyset
for all distinct
b,b^{\prime}\in[a_{1}]
.
For each
i\in[a_{1}]
, we have

|U_{i}\triangle V_{i}|\leq|(1\pm\lambda)m-m_{i}|\leq\lambda m+1.

For each
\hat{\mathbf{x}}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\hat{A}(2,1,\mathbf{a})
, let

\hat{Q}^{(1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}):=U_{\alpha_{1}}\cup U_{\alpha_{2}}
.
Note that
\{\mathcal{K}_{2}(\hat{Q}^{(1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})):\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(2,1,\mathbf{a})\}
forms a partition of
\mathcal{K}_{2}(\mathscr{Q}^{(1)})
.

Now, we inductively construct
\mathscr{Q}^{(2)},\dots,\mathscr{Q}^{(k-1)}
in this order.
Assume that for some
j\in[k]\setminus\{1\}
, we have already defined
\{\mathscr{Q}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{j-1}
with
\mathscr{Q}^{(i)}=\{Q^{(i)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b):\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(i,i-1,\mathbf{a}),b\in[a_{i}]\}
and
\hat{\mathscr{Q}}^{(i)}=\{\hat{Q}^{(i)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}):\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(i+1,i,\mathbf{a})\}
for each
i\in[j-1]
such that the following hold.

(Q1)
j-1

For each
i\in[j-1]
,
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(i,i-1,\mathbf{a})
and
b\in[a_{i}]
,
we have
|P^{(i)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\triangle Q^{(i)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)|\leq 2^{i}i!\lambda n^{i}
.

(Q2)
j-1

For each
i\in[j-1]\setminus\{1\}
and
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(i,i-1,\mathbf{a})
, the collection

\{Q^{(i)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b):b\in[a_{i}]\}
forms a partition of
\mathcal{K}_{i}(\hat{Q}^{(i-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))
.

(Q3)
j-1

For each
i\in[j-1]
and
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(i+1,i,\mathbf{a})
,
we have
\hat{Q}^{(i)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\leq_{i,i-1}\hat{\mathbf{x}}}Q^{(i)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{x}^{(i)}_{\mathbf{y}^{(1)}_{*}})
.

Note that
\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}
satisfies (Q1)
1
–(Q3)
1
. Suppose first that
j\leq k-1
. In this case we will define
\mathscr{Q}^{(j)}
satisfying (Q1)
j
–(Q3)
j
.
For each
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a})
and
b\in[a_{j}]
, we define

\displaystyle Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}P^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\cap\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{Q}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))&\text{ if }b\in[a_{j}-1],\\
\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{Q}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))\setminus\bigcup_{b\in[a_{j}-1]}P^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)&\text{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Let

\mathscr{Q}^{(j)}:=\{Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b):\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}),b\in[a_{j}]\}.

Then for any fixed
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a})
, it is obvious that

Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},1),\dots,Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},a_{j})
forms a partition of
\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{Q}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))
. Thus (Q2)
j
holds.

For each
\hat{\mathbf{z}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a})
, let

\displaystyle\hat{Q}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}):=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{z}}}Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{z}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{y}^{(1)}_{*}}).

Then (Q3)
j
also holds.

Note that for any fixed
(j-1)
-set
J^{\prime}\in\hat{P}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})\triangle\hat{Q}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})
, there are at most
(1+\lambda)m
distinct
j
-sets in
\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{P}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))\triangle\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{Q}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))
containing
J^{\prime}
.
Thus for
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a})
and
b\in[a_{j}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle|P^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\triangle Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{P}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))\triangle\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{Q}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\lambda)m|\hat{P}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})\triangle\hat{Q}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:hatPconsistsofP(x,b)},{\rm(Q3)}_{j-1}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\leq_{j-1,j-2}\hat{\mathbf{x}}}2m|P^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{x}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{y}^{(1)}_{*}})\triangle Q^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{x}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{y}^{(1)}_{*}})|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm(Q1)}_{j-1}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle 2^{j}j!\lambda n^{j}.

Thus (Q1)
j
holds and
we obtain
\{\mathscr{Q}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{j}
satisfying (Q1)
j
–(Q3)
j
.
Inductively we obtain
\mathscr{Q}=\{\mathscr{Q}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}
satisfying (Q1)
k-1
–(Q3)
k-1
.

Note that since
R=(\varepsilon/3,\mathbf{a},d_{\mathbf{a},k})
is a regularity instance, we have the following inequality.

\varepsilon\leq\|\mathbf{a}\|_{\infty}^{-4^{k}}\varepsilon_{\ref{lem: counting}}(\|\mathbf{a}\|^{-1}_{\infty},\|\mathbf{a}\|^{-1}_{\infty},k-1,k).

Thus Lemma
5.4
(together with Lemma
5.5
(ii)) implies for any
j\in[k-1]\setminus\{1\}
and
(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a})\times[a_{j}]
that

|P^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)|\geq\varepsilon^{1/2}n^{j}.

This with (Q1)
k-1
shows that for any
j\in[k-1]\setminus\{1\}
and
(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a})\times[a_{j}]
, the
j
-graph
Q^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)
is nonempty.
Together with properties (Q2)
k-1
and (Q3)
k-1
this in turn ensures that we can apply Lemma
4.2
to show that
\mathscr{Q}
is a family of partitions.

By (Q1)
k-1
and the assumption that
R=(\varepsilon/3,\mathbf{a},d_{\mathbf{a},k})
is a regularity instance, we can apply Lemma
5.11
with
\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q},H^{(k)},H^{(k)},\lambda,\lambda^{9/10}
playing the roles of
\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q},H^{(k)},G^{(k)},\lambda,\nu
to obtain that
\mathscr{Q}=\mathscr{Q}(k-1,\mathbf{a})
is an
(\varepsilon+\lambda^{1/10},\mathbf{a},d_{\mathbf{a},k})
-equitable partition of
H^{(k)}
.
∎

6.
Regular approximations of partitions and hypergraphs

The main aim of this section is to prove a strengthening of the partition version (Lemma
4.1
) of the regular approximation lemma. As described in Section
2.3
, Lemma
4.1
outputs for a given equitable family of partitions
\mathscr{Q}
another family of partitions
\mathscr{P}
that refines
\mathscr{Q}
.
In Lemma
6.1

\mathscr{P}
has the additional feature that it almost refines a further given (arbitrary) family of partitions
\mathscr{O}
.
Observe that we cannot hope to refine
\mathscr{O}
itself, as for example some sets in
\mathscr{O}^{(1)}
may be very small.
We also prove two further tools: Lemma
6.2
allows us to transfer the large scale structure of a hypergraph to another one on a different vertex set and Lemma
6.3
concerns suitable perturbations of a given partition.
Lemmas
6.1
–
6.3
will all be used in the proof of Lemma
7.1
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For all
k,o\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
, all
\eta,\nu>0
,
and every function
\varepsilon:\mathbb{N}^{k-1}\rightarrow(0,1]
,
there are
\mu=\mu_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k,o,s,\eta,\nu,\varepsilon)>0

and
t=t_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k,o,s,\eta,\nu,\varepsilon)\in\mathbb{N}
and
n_{0}=n_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k,o,s,\eta,\nu,\varepsilon)\in\mathbb{N}
such that the following hold.
Suppose

(O1)
6.1

V
is a set and
|V|=n\geq n_{0}
,

(O2)
6.1

\mathscr{O}(k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})=\{\mathscr{O}^{(j)}\}_{j=1}^{k-1}
is an
o
-bounded family of partitions on
V
,

(O3)
6.1

\mathscr{Q}=\mathscr{Q}(k,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
is a
(1/a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}},\mu,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
-equitable
o
-bounded family of partitions on
V
, and

(O4)
6.1

\mathscr{H}^{(k)}=\{H^{(k)}_{1},\dots,H^{(k)}_{s}\}
is a partition of
\binom{V}{k}
so that
\mathscr{H}^{(k)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(k)}
.

Then there exist a family of partitions
\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}(k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
and a partition
\mathscr{G}^{(k)}=\{G^{(k)}_{1},\dots,G^{(k)}_{s}\}
of
\binom{V}{k}
satisfying the following for each
j\in[k-1]
and
i\in[s]
.

(P1)
6.1

\mathscr{P}
is a
t
-bounded
(\eta,\varepsilon(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}),\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
-equitable family of partitions, and
a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{j}
divides
a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j}
,

(P2)
6.1

\mathscr{P}^{(j)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(j)}
and
\mathscr{P}^{(j)}\prec_{\nu}\mathscr{O}^{(j)}
,

(G1)
6.1

G^{(k)}_{i}
is perfectly
\varepsilon(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
-regular with respect to
\mathscr{P}
,

(G2)
6.1

\sum_{i=1}^{s}|G_{i}^{(k)}\triangle H^{(k)}_{i}|\leq\nu\binom{n}{k}
, and

(G3)
6.1

\mathscr{G}^{(k)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(k)}
and if
H^{(k)}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{k}(\mathscr{Q}^{(1)})
then
G_{i}^{(k)}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{k}(\mathscr{Q}^{(1)})
.

We believe that Lemma
6.1
will have additional applications. As we mentioned it is also used in the proof Corollary 9.3 in
[
17
]
.

In Lemma
6.1
we may assume without loss of generality that
1/\mu,t,n_{0}
are non-decreasing in
k,o,s
and non-increasing in
\eta,\nu
.

To prove Lemma
6.1
we proceed by induction on
k
.
In the induction step,
we first construct an ‘intermediate’ family of partitions
\{\mathscr{L}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}
which
satisfies (P1)
6.1
and (P2)
6.1
.
The partitions
\mathscr{L}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathscr{L}^{(k-1)}
are constructed via the inductive assumption of Lemma
6.1
(see Claim
2
).
We then construct a partition
\mathscr{L}^{(k)}
via appropriate applications of the slicing lemma (see Claim
3
).
Finally, we apply Lemma
4.1
with
\mathscr{L}_{*}=\{\mathscr{L}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k}
playing the role of
\mathscr{Q}
to obtain our desired family of partitions
\mathscr{P}
and construct
G_{i}^{(k)}
based on the
k
-graphs guaranteed by Lemma
4.1
.

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

First of all,
by decreasing the value of
\eta
if necessary,
we may assume that
\eta<1/(10k!)
.
We may also assume that
\nu\leq\eta
.

We use induction on
k
. For each
k\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}
, let
L_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k)
be the statement of the lemma. Let
L_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(1)
be the following statement (Claim
1
).

Claim 1

(
L_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(1)
)
.

For all
o,s\in\mathbb{N}
, all
\eta,\nu>0
,
there are
t=t_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(1,o,s,\nu):=so\lceil 2\nu^{-2}\rceil
and
n_{0}=n_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(1,o,s,\nu)\in\mathbb{N}
such that the following hold.
Suppose

(O1)
{}^{1}_{\ref{lem:refreg}}

V
is a set and
|V|=n\geq n_{0}
,

(O2)
{}^{1}_{\ref{lem:refreg}}

\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}
is an equipartition of
V
into
a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}\leq o
parts,

(O3)
{}^{1}_{\ref{lem:refreg}}

\mathscr{H}^{(1)}=\{H^{(1)}_{1},\dots,H^{(1)}_{s}\}
is a partition of
V
so that
\mathscr{H}^{(1)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}
.

Then there exists a partition
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
of
V
satisfying the following.

(P1)
{}^{1}_{\ref{lem:refreg}}

\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
is an equipartition of
V
into
a_{1}^{\mathscr{P}}\leq t
parts, and
a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}
divides
a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}
,

(P2)
{}^{1}_{\ref{lem:refreg}}

\mathscr{P}^{(1)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}
and
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}\prec_{\nu^{2}}\mathscr{H}^{(1)}
.

Proof.

Write
\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}=\{Q^{(1)}_{i}:i\in[a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}}]\}.
Let
a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}:=sa^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}\lceil 2\nu^{-2}\rceil
,
let
m:=\min\{|Q^{(1)}_{i}|:i\in[a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}}]\}
, and let
m^{\prime}:=\lfloor|V|/a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}\rfloor
.
Thus
|Q^{(1)}_{i}|\in\{m,m+1\}
for each
i\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}]
.
The sets in
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
will have size
m^{\prime}
or
m^{\prime}+1
.
Note that

\displaystyle m^{\prime}\cdot\frac{a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}}{a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}}\leq\left\lfloor\frac{|V|}{a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}}\cdot\frac{a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}}{a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}}\right\rfloor=m<(m^{\prime}+1)\cdot\frac{a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}}{a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}}.

In particular, as
\frac{a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}}{a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}}
is an integer, we have

(6.1)

\displaystyle 0\leq m~({\rm mod}~m^{\prime})\leq\frac{a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}}{a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}}.

To obtain
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
we further (almost) refine
\mathscr{H}^{(1)}
.
For each
i\in[s]
, we define
\ell_{i}:=\lfloor|H^{(1)}_{i}|/m^{\prime}\rfloor
.
We arbitrarily partition
H^{(1)}_{i}
into
\mathscr{L}(i,0),\dots,\mathscr{L}(i,\ell_{i})
such that
|\mathscr{L}(i,r)|=m^{\prime}
for all
r\in[\ell_{i}]
and
|\mathscr{L}(i,0)|<m^{\prime}
.
For each
j\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}]
, let
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,0):=\bigcup_{H^{(1)}_{i}\subseteq Q^{(1)}_{j}}\mathscr{L}(i,0)
.
Let
\ell^{\prime}_{j}:=\lfloor|\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,0)|/m^{\prime}\rfloor
.
We arbitrarily partition
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,0)
into
\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}(j,0),\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,1),\dots,\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,\ell^{\prime}_{j})
such that

|\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,r)|=m^{\prime}
for all
r\in[\ell^{\prime}_{j}]
and
|\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}(j,0)|<m^{\prime}
.
Note that since
\mathscr{H}^{(1)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}
, for all
j\in[a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}}]
, we have

Q^{(1)}_{j}=\bigcup_{i\colon H^{(1)}_{i}\subseteq Q^{(1)}_{j}}H^{(1)}_{i}=\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}(j,0)\cup\bigcup_{r\in[\ell^{\prime}_{j}]}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,r)\cup\bigcup_{i\colon H^{(1)}_{i}\subseteq Q^{(1)}_{j},r\in[\ell_{i}]}\mathscr{L}(i,r).

As all sets
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,r)
and
\mathscr{L}(i,r)
with
r>0
have size exactly
m^{\prime}
, the fact that
|\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}(j,0)|<m^{\prime}
implies that we have
|\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}(j,0)|=m~({\rm mod}~m^{\prime})
.
On the other hand, this together with (
6.1
) implies that

|\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}(j,0)|\leq a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}=\ell^{\prime}_{j}+\sum_{i\colon H^{(1)}_{i}\subseteq Q^{(1)}_{j}}\ell_{i}.

Hence, by distributing at most one vertex from
\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}(j,0)
into each of the sets in
\{\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,r):r\in[\ell^{\prime}_{j}]\}\cup\{\mathscr{L}(i,r):H^{(1)}_{i}\subseteq Q^{(1)}_{j},r\in[\ell_{i}]\}
,
we can obtain the following collection

\{L^{(1)}(j,1),\dots,L^{(1)}(j,a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1})\}

of sets of size
m^{\prime}
or
m^{\prime}+1
, which forms an equipartition of
Q^{(1)}_{j}
.
Let

\mathscr{P}^{(1)}:=\{L^{(1)}(j,r):j\in[a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}}],r\in[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}]\}.

Then (P1)
{}^{1}_{\ref{lem:refreg}}
holds.
By construction, for each
L^{(1)}\in\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
, either there exists
(i,r)\in[s]\times[\ell_{i}]
such that
|L^{(1)}\setminus\mathscr{L}(i,r)|\leq 1
or there exists
(j,r)\in[a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}}]\times[\ell^{\prime}_{j}]
such that
|L^{(1)}\setminus\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,r)|\leq 1
.
In the former case, let
f(L^{(1)}):=H^{(1)}_{i}
and the latter case, let
f(L^{(1)})
be an arbitrary set in
\mathscr{H}^{(1)}
.
Then

\displaystyle\sum_{L^{(1)}\in\mathscr{P}^{(1)}}|L^{(1)}\setminus f(L^{(1)})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathscr{P}^{(1)}|+\sum_{(j,r)\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}]\times[\ell_{j}^{\prime}]}|\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(j,r)|\leq a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}+\sum_{j\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}],H^{(1)}_{i}\subseteq Q^{(1)}_{j}}|\mathscr{L}(i,0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}+sa^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}m^{\prime}\leq\nu^{2}|V|.

The final inequality follows since
n\geq n_{0}
.
This and the construction of
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
shows that

\mathscr{P}^{(1)}\prec_{\nu^{2}}\mathscr{H}^{(1)}
and
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}.

This shows that (P2)
{}^{1}_{\ref{lem:refreg}}
holds and thus completes the proof of Claim
1
.
∎

So assume that
k\geq 2
and
L_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k-1)
holds.
Let
\mu_{\ref{RAL(k)}},t_{\ref{RAL(k)}},n_{\ref{RAL(k)}}
be the functions defined in Lemma
4.1
.
By decreasing the value of
\varepsilon(\mathbf{a})
if necessary, we may assume that for all
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{k-1}
, we have

(6.2)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\mathbf{a})\ll 1/s,1/k,1/\|\mathbf{a}\|_{\infty}.

If
k=2
, let
T:=o^{4^{k}+1}\lceil 2\nu^{-2}\rceil
.
If
k\geq 3
, for each
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{k-2}
, let
T=T(\mathbf{a},o,\nu)=\max\{\|\mathbf{a}\|_{\infty},o^{4^{k}+1}\lceil 2\nu^{-2}\rceil\}.

If
k\geq 3
, then we also let
\mu^{\prime}:\mathbb{N}^{k-2}\rightarrow(0,1]
be a function such that for any
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{k-2}
, we have

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}(\mathbf{a})\ll\nu,1/k,1/o,1/\|\mathbf{a}\|_{\infty}\kern 5.0pt\text{ and }\kern 5.0pt\mu^{\prime}(\mathbf{a})<(\mu_{\ref{RAL(k)}}(k,T,2sT^{2^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\varepsilon^{2}))^{2}.

For all
k\geq 2
, let

\displaystyle t_{k-1}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}so^{4^{k}+1}\lceil 2\nu^{-2}\rceil&\text{ if }k=2,\\
\max\{t_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k-1,o,o^{4^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\mu^{\prime}),o^{4^{k}+1}\lceil 2\nu^{-2}\rceil\}&\text{ if }k\geq 3,\end{array}\right.

which exists by the induction hypothesis.
Choose an integer
t
such that

(6.6)

\displaystyle 1/t\ll 1/t_{\ref{RAL(k)}}(k,t_{k-1},2st_{k-1}^{2^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\varepsilon^{2}),1/t_{k-1},

and choose
\mu>0
such that

\displaystyle\mu\ll\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1/t,\mu_{\ref{RAL(k)}}(k,T,2sT^{2^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\varepsilon^{2})&\text{ if }k=2,\\
1/t,\mu^{\prime}(\mathbf{a}),\varepsilon(\mathbf{a}^{\prime}),\mu_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k-1,o,o^{4^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\mu^{\prime})&\text{ if }k\geq 3.\\
\text{ for any }\mathbf{a}\in[t]^{k-2},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\in[t]^{k-1}&\end{array}\right.

Finally, choose an integer
n_{0}
such that

\displaystyle 1/n_{0}\ll\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1/n_{\ref{RAL(k)}}(k,t_{k-1},2st_{k-1}^{2^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\varepsilon^{2}),1/n_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k-1,o,o^{4^{k}},\nu),1/\mu.&\text{ if }k=2,\\
1/n_{\ref{RAL(k)}}(k,t_{k-1},2st_{k-1}^{2^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\varepsilon^{2}),1/n_{\ref{lem:refreg}}(k-1,o,o^{4^{k}},\eta,\nu/3,\mu^{\prime}),1/\mu&\text{ if }k\geq 3.\end{array}\right.

Suppose
\mathscr{O}(k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
,
\mathscr{Q}(k,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
and
\mathscr{H}^{(k)}
are given (families of) partitions satisfying (O1)
6.1
–(O4)
6.1
with
\mu,t,n_{0}
as defined above.
Write

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}^{(k-1)}

\displaystyle=\{O^{(k-1)}_{1},\dots,O^{(k-1)}_{s_{O}}\}\kern 5.0pt\text{ and }\kern 5.0pt\mathscr{Q}^{(k-1)}=\{Q^{(k-1)}_{1},\dots,Q^{(k-1)}_{s_{Q}}\}.

Let

(6.12)

\displaystyle O^{(k-1)}_{s_{O}+1}

\displaystyle:=\binom{V}{k-1}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\mathscr{O}^{(1)}),\kern 5.0pt\kern 5.0ptQ^{(k-1)}_{s_{Q}+1}:=\binom{V}{k-1}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}^{(k-1)}

\displaystyle:=\{O^{(k-1)}_{i}\cap Q^{(k-1)}_{j}:i\in[s_{O}+1],j\in[s_{Q}+1]\}\setminus\{\emptyset\}.

We also write
\mathscr{R}^{(k-1)}=\{R^{(k-1)}_{1},\dots,R^{(k-1)}_{s^{\prime\prime}}\}.

\displaystyle s^{\prime\prime}\leq\left(o^{2^{k}}\right)^{2}\leq o^{4^{k}}.

Claim 2
.

\{\mathscr{L}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k-1}
and

Since

, this in turn implies

–
(Lenumi3)
.

Now we assume

. First, we apply

Subclaim 1
.

For each
i\in[s^{\prime}]
and for each

,
the
(k-1)
-graph

(\mu^{\prime}(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{L}}),d^{\prime}_{g(j)})
-regular with respect to
\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i}
, where

.

Proof.

First, note that since
\mathscr{L}\prec\{\mathscr{Q}^{(j)}\}_{j=1}^{k-2}
, one of the following holds.

(LL1)

There exists

\hat{Q}^{(k-2)}\in\hat{\mathscr{Q}}^{(k-2)}
such that
\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i}\subseteq\hat{Q}^{(k-2)}
.

(LL2)

\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{L_{i}}^{(k-2)})\subseteq\binom{V}{k-1}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\mathscr{Q}^{(1)})
.

If
(LL1)
holds, then by (L
′
1) and the fact that
\mu\ll\mu^{\prime}(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{L}})\ll\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{L}}\|_{\infty}^{-k}

we can apply Lemma
5.4
twice to conclude that

|\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i})|\geq t_{k-1}^{-2^{k}}|\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{Q}^{(k-2)})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: t def},\eqref{eq: mu def}}}{{\geq}}\mu^{1/3}|\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{Q}^{(k-2)})|.

Note that, for each
j\in J(i)
,

is
(\mu,1/a_{k-1}^{\mathscr{Q}})
-regular with respect to
\hat{Q}^{(k-2)}
.
Together with Lemma

(ii) this implies that

is
(\mu^{2/3},1/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{k-1})
-regular with respect to
\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i}
for each
j\in J(i)
.

If
(LL2)
holds,
j\in J(i)
implies that

.
Thus (
6.12
) means that

, which implies
g(j)=s_{Q}+1
.
Also

implies that
\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{L}_{i}^{(k-2)})\subseteq Q^{(k-1)}_{s_{Q}+1}.

Thus
Q^{(k-1)}_{s_{Q}+1}
is
(0,1)
-regular with respect to
\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i}
.
This completes the proof of Subclaim
1
.
∎

Moreover, (M
′
1) implies that for each

\displaystyle a^{\mathscr{L}}_{k-1}:=a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{k-1}o^{4^{k}}\lceil 2\nu^{-2}\rceil,

Let
\ell_{i,j}:=\lfloor d(\mathscr{L}(i,j)\mid\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i})a^{\mathscr{L}}_{k-1}\rfloor
, so

if

.

) to

for each

.
We obtain edge-disjoint

(

Then

(6.18)

\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p)

Indeed the union lemma (Lemma

) (applied with

,

So Lemma

(iii) implies that (

) holds
where

In addition, for all

and

, we have

(6.19)

\displaystyle|\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathscr{L}3)}}{{\leq}}|g^{-1}(p)|\cdot\frac{5}{4a^{\mathscr{L}}_{k-1}}\cdot|\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq: size s''}),(\ref{eq: def aLk-1})}}{{\leq}}\nu^{2}|\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i})\cap Q^{(k-1)}_{p}|.

Again, we apply the slicing lemma (Lemma

), this time to
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p)
.
By (
6.18
),
we obtain edge-disjoint
(k-1)
-graphs

(

,

(
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
2)

is
(\mu^{\prime}(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{L}})^{1/2},1/a^{\mathscr{L}}_{k-1})
-regular with respect to

for each
\ell\in[\ell^{\prime}_{i,p}]
.

Thus for each
i\in[s^{\prime}]
, (
6.15
), (
\mathscr{L}1
) and (
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}1
) imply that

\displaystyle\bigcup_{p\in g(J(i))}\left(\{\mathscr{L}(i,j,r):j,r\text{ with }g(j)=p,r\in[\ell_{i,j}]\}\cup\{\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p,1),\dots,\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p,\ell_{i,p}^{\prime})\}\right)

forms a partition of

into

,
where the latter follows from
(

2)
and (
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}2
).
We define

\mathscr{L}^{(k-1)}:=\bigcup_{i\in[s^{\prime}],j\in J(i)}\{\mathscr{L}(i,j,r):r\in[\ell_{i,j}]\}\cup\bigcup_{i\in[s^{\prime}],p\in g(J(i))}\{\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p,\ell):\ell\in[\ell^{\prime}_{i,p}]\}.

Then

follows from (L

1) and the construction of

(

-boundedness follows by (

)).
Note that for all
i\in[s^{\prime}]
,

,

,

holds.

Subclaim 2
.

\mathscr{L}^{(k-1)}\prec_{\nu/2}\mathscr{O}^{(k-1)}
.

Proof.

To prove the subclaim, we define a suitable function
f_{k-1}:\mathscr{L}^{(k-1)}\to\mathscr{O}^{(k-1)}
.
For each
j\in[s^{\prime\prime}]
,
let
h(j)\in[s_{O}+1]
be the index such that
R^{(k-1)}_{j}=O^{(k-1)}_{h(j)}\cap Q^{(k-1)}_{p}
for some
p\in[s_{Q}+1]
.
For each

i\in[s^{\prime}]
,
j\in J(i)
,

r\in[\ell_{i,j}]
,
\ell\in[\ell^{\prime}_{i,g(j)}]
, let

f_{k-1}(\mathscr{L}(i,j,r)):=O^{(k-1)}_{h(j)}\text{ and }f_{k-1}(\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,g(j),\ell)):=O^{(k-1)}_{h(j)}.

For fixed
j\in[s^{\prime\prime}]
, (
6.15
) and
(

L

1)
imply that

(6.20)

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i\in[s^{\prime}],r\in[\ell_{i,j}]}\mathscr{L}(i,j,r)\subseteq M^{(k-1)}_{j}.

Hence

\displaystyle\hskip-68.28644pt\sum_{L^{(k-1)}\in\mathscr{L}^{(k-1)}}|L^{(k-1)}\setminus f_{k-1}(L^{(k-1)})|\leq\sum_{i,j,r}|\mathscr{L}(i,j,r)\setminus f_{k-1}(\mathscr{L}(i,j,r))|+\sum_{i,p,\ell}|\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p,\ell)|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Lijr},(\mathscr{L}^{\prime}1)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[s^{\prime\prime}]}|M^{(k-1)}_{j}\setminus O^{(k-1)}_{h(j)}|+\sum_{i,p}|\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(i,p)|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: size of sL'(i,p)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[s^{\prime\prime}]}|M^{(k-1)}_{j}\setminus R^{(k-1)}_{j}|+\nu^{2}\sum_{i,p}|\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{L}^{(k-2)}_{i})\cap Q^{(k-1)}_{p}|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm(M}^{\prime}{\rm 2)}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{\nu}{3}\binom{n}{k-1}+\nu^{2}\binom{n}{k-1}\leq\frac{2\nu}{5}\binom{n}{k-1}.

The fact that
a_{1}^{\mathscr{L}}\geq\eta^{-1}
and (
2.5
) together imply that
|\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\mathscr{L}^{(1)})|\geq\frac{4}{5}\binom{n}{k-1}
, so the subclaim follows.
∎

This shows that
(Lenumi3)
holds and completes the proof of Claim
2
.
∎

Claim 3
.

For each

Let

\mathscr{L}^{(k)}:=\{L^{(k)}(i,r):i\in[\hat{s}_{\mathscr{L}}],r\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{k}]\},\kern 5.0pt\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{L}_{*}}:=(a^{\mathscr{L}}_{1},\dots,a^{\mathscr{L}}_{k-1},a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{k})\kern 5.0pt\text{ and }\kern 5.0pt\mathscr{L}_{*}:=\{\mathscr{L}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{k},

(6.21)

\displaystyle\{J^{(k)}_{1},\dots,J^{(k)}_{s_{J}}\}:=\mathscr{J}^{(k)},\text{ and }J^{\prime}(i):=\{j^{\prime}\in[s_{J}]:J^{(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\subseteq H^{(k)}_{i}\}\text{ for each }i\in[s].

(6.24)

), the union lemma (Lemma

By the definition of

holds.

We now verify (G2)
6.1
.
Consider any edge
e\in G^{(k)}_{i}\setminus H^{(k)}_{i}
for some
i\in[s]
.
We claim that

(6.25)

\displaystyle e\in\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in[s_{J}]}J^{(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\setminus G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}.

To prove (
6.25
) note that
since
\mathscr{J}^{(k)}
is a partition of
\binom{V}{k}
, there exists
j^{\prime}\in[s_{J}]
such that
e\in J^{(k)}_{j^{\prime}}
. So (
6.25
) holds if
e\notin G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}
.
Thus assume for a contradiction that
e\in G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}
.
Let
i_{*}\in[s]
be the index such that
j^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}(i_{*})
.
Then
J^{(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\subseteq H^{(k)}_{i_{*}}
.

Since
\{H^{\prime}(1),\dots,H^{\prime}(s_{Q}+1)\}
forms a partition of
[s]
,
there exists
i^{\prime}\in[s_{Q}+1]
such that
i_{*}\in H^{\prime}(i^{\prime})
.
Thus
e\in J^{(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\subseteq H^{(k)}_{i_{*}}\subseteq Q^{(k)}_{i^{\prime}}
.
Hence
Q^{(k)}_{i^{\prime}}\cap G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
.
Since
\mathscr{G}^{\prime(k)}\prec\mathscr{Q}^{(k)}
,
this implies that
G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\subseteq Q^{(k)}_{i^{\prime}}
and so
j^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}(i^{\prime})
.
Consequently, we have
i_{*}\in H^{\prime}(i^{\prime})
and
j^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}(i_{*})\cap G^{\prime}(i^{\prime})
. This together with (
6.24
) implies that
e\in G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\subseteq G^{(k)}_{i_{*}}
. Since
\mathscr{G}^{(k)}
is a partition of
\binom{V}{k}
, this implies that
i=i_{*}
.
But then
e\in H^{(k)}_{i_{*}}=H^{(k)}_{i}
, a contradiction.
This proves (
6.25
).

Then

(6.26)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[s]}|G^{(k)}_{i}\setminus H^{(k)}_{i}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:einJG})}}{{\leq}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[s_{J}]}|J^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\setminus G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}|.

Since all of
\mathscr{H}^{(k)},\mathscr{G}^{(k)},\mathscr{J}^{(k)}
and
\mathscr{G}^{\prime{(k)}}
are partitions of
\binom{V}{k}
, we obtain

\sum_{i\in[s]}|G^{(k)}_{i}\triangle H^{(k)}_{i}|=2\sum_{i\in[s]}|G^{(k)}_{i}\setminus H^{(k)}_{i}|,\text{ and }\sum_{i\in[s_{J}]}|G^{\prime(k)}_{i}\triangle J^{(k)}_{i}|=2\sum_{i\in[s_{J}]}|J^{(k)}_{i}\setminus G^{\prime(k)}_{i}|.

Thus we conclude

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[s]}|G^{(k)}_{i}\triangle H^{(k)}_{i}|=2\sum_{i\in[s]}|G^{(k)}_{i}\setminus H^{(k)}_{i}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:GHJ})}}{{\leq}}2\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[s_{J}]}|J^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}\setminus G^{\prime(k)}_{j^{\prime}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\ref{eq:P'4}}}}{{\leq}}\nu\binom{n}{k}.

Thus (G2)
6.1
holds.
∎

Suppose we are given a
(k-1)
-graph
H
on a vertex set
V
.
In the next lemma we apply Lemma
4.1
to show that, given a different vertex set

,
there exists another

-graph

on

whose large scale structure is very close to that of

.

Lemma 6.2
.

Suppose
0<1/m,1/n\ll\varepsilon\ll\nu,1/o,1/k\leq 1
and
k,o\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}
.
Suppose
\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}(k-1,\mathbf{a})
and
\mathscr{Q}=\mathscr{Q}(k-1,\mathbf{a})
are both

-bounded
(1/a_{1},\varepsilon,\mathbf{a})
-equitable families of partitions of

and
V^{\prime}
respectively with

and
|V^{\prime}|=m
.
Suppose that
H^{(k-1)}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\mathscr{P}^{(1)})
. Then there exists a
(k-1)
-graph
G^{(k-1)}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\mathscr{P}^{(1)})
on
V
and a
(k-1)
-graph

on

such that

(F1)
6.2

|H^{(k-1)}\triangle G^{(k-1)}|\leq\nu\binom{n}{k-1}
,

(F2)
6.2

d(\mathcal{K}_{k}(G^{(k-1)})\mid\hat{P}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}))=d(\mathcal{K}_{k}(F^{(k-1)})\mid\hat{Q}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}))\pm\nu

for each
\hat{\mathbf{z}}\in\hat{A}(k,k-1,\mathbf{a})
.

To prove Lemma
6.2
,
we first apply Lemma
4.1

to obtain a family of partitions
\mathscr{R}=\mathscr{R}(k-2,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{R}})
and a
k
-graph
G^{(k-1)}
as in (F1)
6.2
.
We then ‘project’
\mathscr{R}
onto
V^{\prime}
(so that it refines
\mathscr{Q}
).
This results in a partition
\mathscr{L}
.
We then apply the slicing lemma to construct
F^{(k-1)}
which respects
\mathscr{L}
(and in particular has the appropriate densities).

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

First suppose
k=2
.
Then
H^{(1)}\subseteq V
.
Let
G^{(1)}:=H^{(1)}
.
Thus (F1)
6.2
holds.
Recall that for each
b\in[a_{1}]
, the vertex sets
P^{(1)}(b,b)
and
Q^{(1)}(b,b)
denote the
b
-th parts in
\mathscr{P}^{(1)}
and
\mathscr{Q}^{(1)}
, respectively.
For each
b\in[a_{1}]
,
let

be a subset of

with

|F^{(1)}(b,b)|=\left\lfloor\frac{m}{n}|H^{(1)}\cap P^{(1)}(b,b)|\right\rfloor

and let
F^{(1)}:=\bigcup_{b\in[a_{1}]}F^{(1)}(b,b)
.
For each
\mathbf{z}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\hat{A}(2,1,\mathbf{a})
, we have

\displaystyle d(\mathcal{K}_{2}(F^{(1)})\mid\hat{Q}^{(1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}))

\displaystyle=\frac{|F^{(1)}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1})||F^{(1)}(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})|}{(m/a_{1}\pm 1)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(|H^{(1)}\cap P^{(1)}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1})|\pm n/m)(|H^{(1)}\cap P^{(1)}(\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})|\pm n/m)}{(n/a_{1}\pm n/m)^{2}}

\displaystyle=d(\mathcal{K}_{2}(G^{(1)})\mid\hat{P}^{(1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}))\pm\nu.

Thus (F2)
6.2
holds.

Now we show the lemma for

.
Let

be a constant such that

.
Let

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}(\mathbf{b})\ll\nu,1/o,1/k,1/\|\mathbf{b}\|_{\infty}\text{ for all }\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{N}^{k-2}.

Let

Since
\varepsilon\ll\nu,1/o,1/k,\eta^{\prime}
, we may assume that

(6.28)

\displaystyle 0<\varepsilon\ll\mu_{\ref{RAL(k)}}(k-1,o,o^{4^{k}},\eta^{\prime},\nu,\varepsilon^{\prime}),1/t,\min\{\varepsilon^{\prime}(\mathbf{b}):\mathbf{b}\in[t]^{k-2}\},

and we may assume that
n,m>n_{0}:=n_{\ref{RAL(k)}}(k-1,o,o^{4^{k}},\eta^{\prime},\nu,\varepsilon^{\prime}).

Let

\displaystyle\{H^{(k-1)}_{1},\dots,H^{(k-1)}_{s}\}

\displaystyle:=\{P^{(k-1)}\cap H^{(k-1)}:P^{(k-1)}\in\mathscr{P}^{(k-1)}\}\setminus\{\emptyset\},

\displaystyle\{H^{(k-1)}_{s+1},\dots,H^{(k-1)}_{s+s^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle:=\left(\{P^{(k-1)}\setminus H^{(k-1)}:P^{(k-1)}\in\mathscr{P}^{(k-1)}\}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt\left.\cup\left\{\binom{V}{k-1}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\mathscr{P}^{(1)})\right\}\right)\setminus\{\emptyset\},

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{(k-1)}

\displaystyle:=\{H^{(k-1)}_{1},\dots,H^{(k-1)}_{s+s^{\prime}}\}.

Hence
\mathscr{H}^{(k-1)}
is a partition of
\binom{V}{k-1}
such that

and

by Proposition

(viii).

.

(6.38)

\displaystyle\hat{P}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}})=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{w}}\leq_{k-1,k-2}\hat{\mathbf{z}}}P^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{z}^{(k-1)}_{\mathbf{w}^{(1)}_{*}}).

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{R}^{(k-2)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{P}^{(k-2)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))\kern 5.0pt\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{R}^{(k-2)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\cap\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{P}^{(k-2)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))=\emptyset\text{ for }\hat{\mathbf{w}}\neq\hat{\mathbf{x}}.

.
Together this implies

\hat{P}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}})\cap\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{R}^{(k-2)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:unionP})}}{{=}}P^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},\mathbf{z}^{(k-1)}_{\mathbf{x}^{(1)}_{*}})\cap\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{R}^{(k-2)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq: Q* P* def})}}{{=}}P_{*}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{z}^{(k-1)}_{\mathbf{x}^{(1)}_{*}}).

\displaystyle G^{(k-1)}\cap\hat{P}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}})\cap\mathcal{K}_{k-1}(\hat{R}^{(k-2)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))=G^{(k-1)}\cap P_{*}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{z}^{(k-1)}_{\mathbf{x}^{(1)}_{*}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: G hat by b def mimic}}}{{=}}G(\hat{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{z}^{(k-1)}_{\mathbf{x}^{(1)}_{*}}).

\displaystyle F^{(k-1)}\cap\hat{Q}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}})

Analogously using (

In particular, this means that

twice with the following objects and parameters, once with

playing the role of

t

\mathbf{a}

k

F

(6.40)

\displaystyle\frac{|\mathcal{K}_{k}(G^{(k-1)}\cap\hat{P}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}))|}{\binom{n}{k}}

\displaystyle=IC(K^{(k-1)}_{k},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{R}},\hat{\mathbf{z}},k-1})\pm\nu^{2}o^{-4^{k}}\kern 5.0pt\text{ and}

(6.41)

\displaystyle\frac{|\mathcal{K}_{k}(F^{(k-1)}\cap\hat{Q}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}}))|}{\binom{m}{k}}

\displaystyle=(1\pm\nu^{2})\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}a_{j}^{-\binom{k}{j}}m^{k}\geq o^{-4^{k}}\binom{m}{k}.

This together with (

) and (

) implies that

∎

Suppose we are given two families of partitions
\mathscr{P},\mathscr{O}
such that
\mathscr{P}
almost refines
\mathscr{O}
and such that
\mathscr{O}
is an equitable partition of some
k
-graph
H
.
Roughly speaking, the next lemma shows that there is a family of partitions
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}

such that
\mathscr{P}\prec\mathscr{O}^{\prime}
and such that
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}
is still an equitable partition of
H
(with a somewhat larger regularity constant).

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose
0<1/m,1/n\ll\varepsilon\ll\nu\ \ll\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
and
k\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}
.
Suppose
V
is a vertex set of size
n
.
Suppose
R=(\varepsilon_{0}/2,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})
is a regularity instance and

\mathscr{O}=\mathscr{O}(k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
is an
(\varepsilon_{0},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})
-equitable partition of a
k
-graph
H^{(k)}
on
V
.
Suppose there exists an
(\eta,\varepsilon,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
-equitable family of partitions
\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}(k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
on
V
such that
\mathscr{P}\prec_{\nu}\mathscr{O}
.
Then there exists a family of partitions
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}
on
V
such that

(O
′
1)
6.3

\mathscr{P}\prec\mathscr{O}^{\prime}
,

(O
′
2)
6.3

\mathscr{O}^{\prime}
is a
(1/a_{1}^{\mathscr{O}},\varepsilon_{0}+\nu^{1/20},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},\nu^{1/20})
-equitable family of partitions and it is an

(\varepsilon_{0}+\nu^{1/20},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})
-partition of
H^{(k)}
,

(O
′
3)
6.3

for each
j\in[k-1]
and
(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})\times[a_{j}^{\mathscr{O}}]
, we have
|O^{\prime(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\triangle O^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)|\leq\nu^{1/2}\binom{n}{j}.

We construct
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}
by induction on
j\in[k-1]
.
When constructing
{\mathscr{O}^{\prime}}^{(j-1)}
a natural approach is as follows.
For a given class
O^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)
of
\mathscr{O}^{(j)}
we can let
O^{\prime(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)
consist e.g. of all classes of
\mathscr{P}^{(j)}
which lie (mostly) in
O^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)
.
This is formalized by the function
f_{j}
in (
6.42
).
However, this construction may not fit with the existing polyads of
\hat{P}^{(j-1)}
(i.e. it may violate Definition
2.1
(ii)).
This issue requires some adjustments, whose overall effect can be shown to be negligible.

Proof of Lemma
6.3
.

For any function
f:\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j}]\rightarrow\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})\times[a^{\mathscr{O}}_{j}]
, let

d(f):=\sum_{(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j}]}|P^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\setminus O^{(j)}(f(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b))|.

Note that
\mathscr{P}\prec_{\nu}\mathscr{O}
implies that for each
j\in[k-1]
,
there exists a function
f_{j}:\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j}]\rightarrow\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})\times[a^{\mathscr{O}}_{j}]
such that

(6.42)

\displaystyle d(f_{j})\leq\nu\binom{n}{j}.

Moreover, note that since
R
is a regularity instance (see Definition
2.9
), we have

7.
Sampling a regular partition

In this section we prove Theorem
3.1
. In Section
7.1
we provide the main tool (Lemma
7.1
) for this result
and in Section
7.2
we deduce Theorem
3.1
.

7.1.
Building a family of partitions from three others

In this subsection we prove our key tool (Lemma
7.1
) for the proof of Theorem
3.1
.
Roughly speaking Lemma
7.1
states the following.
Suppose there are two
k
-graphs
H_{1},H_{2}
with vertex set
V_{1},V_{2}
, respectively, and there are two
\varepsilon
-equitable families of partitions of these
k
-graphs which
have the same parameters.
Suppose further that there is another
\varepsilon_{0}
-equitable family of partitions
\mathscr{O}_{1}
for
H_{1}
.
Then there is an equitable family of partitions
\mathscr{O}_{2}
of
H_{2}
which has the (roughly) same parameters as
\mathscr{O}_{1}
provided
\varepsilon\ll\varepsilon_{0}
.
Even more loosely,
the result says that if two hypergraphs share a single ‘high-quality’ regularity partition, then they share any ‘low-quality’ regularity partition.

Lemma 7.1
.

Suppose
0<1/n,1/m\ll\varepsilon\ll 1/T,1/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1}\ll\delta\ll\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
and
k\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}
.
Suppose
\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}}\in[T]^{k-1}
.
Suppose that
R=(\varepsilon_{0}/2,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})
is a regularity instance.
Suppose
V_{1},V_{2}
are sets of size
n,m
, and

are
k
-graphs on

, respectively.
Suppose

(P1)
7.1

,

, and

Then there exists an

A crucial point here is that the construction of

For an illustration of the proof strategy of Lemma

Our strategy is first to apply Lemma

to obtain a family of partitions

\mathscr{O}
,
\mathscr{Q}
and
\mathscr{H}^{(k)}
, respectively,
satisfy (O1)
6.1
–(O4)
6.1
in Lemma
6.1
.
We obtain a family of partitions
\mathscr{P}_{1}=\mathscr{P}_{1}(k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
such that the following hold.

(P11)

\mathscr{P}_{1}
is
(1/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1},\overline{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}),\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
-equitable and
t^{\prime}
-bounded, and
a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{j}
divides
a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j}
for each
j\in[k-1]
.

(P12)

\mathscr{P}_{1}^{(j)}\prec\mathscr{Q}_{1}^{(j)}
and
\mathscr{P}_{1}^{(j)}\prec_{\nu}\mathscr{O}_{1}^{(j)}
for each
j\in[k-1]
.

Let

\varepsilon^{\prime}:=\overline{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}),\kern 5.0pt\text{ and }\kern 5.0pta_{k}^{\mathscr{P}}=a_{k}^{\mathscr{Q}}=a_{k}^{\mathscr{O}}:=1.

Hence
\varepsilon^{\prime}\ll\nu,\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}\|_{\infty}^{-k}
by the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
.

By
(P12)
, for each
j\in[k-1]
,
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
, and
b^{\prime}\in[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j}]
,
either there exists
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
and
b\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{j}]
such that
P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b^{\prime})\subseteq Q_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)

or
P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b^{\prime})\cap Q_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)=\emptyset
for all
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
and
b\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{j}]
.
This allows us to describe
\mathscr{P}_{1}
in terms of
\mathscr{Q}_{1}
in the following way.
For each
j\in[k-1]
,
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
, and
b\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{j}]
, we define

\displaystyle A_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)

\displaystyle:=\{(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b^{\prime})\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j}]:P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b^{\prime})\subseteq Q_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\}\text{ and}

(7.1)

\displaystyle A_{j}

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}}),b\in[a_{j}^{\mathscr{Q}}]}A_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b).

For each
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(k,k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
, let

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{k}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle:=\{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(k,k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}):\hat{P}^{(k-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}})\subseteq\hat{Q}^{(k-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})\},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(k,k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})}\hat{A}_{k}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}).

The density function
d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}},k}
for
\mathscr{Q}_{1}
naturally gives rise to a density function for
\mathscr{P}_{1}
.
Indeed, for each
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in A(k,k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
, we define

d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}},k}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}},k}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})&\text{ if }\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}_{k}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})\text{ for some $\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(k,k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})$ and}\\
0&\text{ if }\hat{\mathbf{y}}\notin\hat{A}_{k}.\end{array}\right.

Recall that
\mathscr{P}_{1}
is a
(1/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1},\varepsilon^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
-equitable family of partitions,

\mathscr{Q}_{1}
is a
(1/a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{1},\varepsilon,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
-equitable family of partitions,
and
\varepsilon\ll\varepsilon^{\prime}\ll\nu,\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}\|_{\infty}^{-k}
.
Thus Lemma
5.4

implies that for each
j\in[k-1]
,
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
, and
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
,
we have

(7.2)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))|=(1\pm\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}n^{j+1}\text{ and }|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{Q}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))|=(1\pm\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}n^{j+1}.

By
(P11)
, for each
j\in[k-2]
,
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
and
b\in[a_{j+1}^{\mathscr{P}}]
, we have

(7.3)

\displaystyle|P^{(j+1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b)|=(1/a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j+1}\pm\overline{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}))|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))|=(1\pm 2\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j+1}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}n^{j+1}.

It will be convenient to restrict our attention to the
k
-graph
G_{1}^{(k)}
which consists of the crossing
k
-sets of
H_{1}^{(k)}

with respect to
\mathscr{Q}_{1}^{(1)}
(rather than
H_{1}^{(k)}
itself).

Claim 1
.

Let
G^{(k)}_{1}:=H^{(k)}_{1}\cap\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}_{k}}\mathcal{K}_{k}(\hat{P}_{1}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))
.
Then

(G11)

\mathscr{P}_{1}
is an
(\varepsilon^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}},k})
-equitable partition of
G_{1}^{(k)}
.

(G12)

|G^{(k)}_{1}\triangle H^{(k)}_{1}|\leq\nu\binom{n}{k}
.

Proof.

Consider
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(k,k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
and
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}_{k}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})
.
Note that (
7.2
) implies that

|\mathcal{K}_{k}(\hat{P}^{(k-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|\mathcal{K}_{k}(\hat{Q}^{(k-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))|.

Also by (P1)
7.1
,
H_{1}^{(k)}
is
(\varepsilon,d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}},k}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))
-regular with respect to
\hat{Q}^{(k-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})
.
Thus by Lemma
5.1
(ii)
H^{(k)}_{1}
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}},k}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))
-regular with respect to
\hat{P}_{1}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}})
. Together with the definition of
d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}},k}(\hat{\mathbf{y}})
this in turn shows that for all
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(k,k-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
we have that

G_{1}^{(k)}
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}},k}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))
-regular with respect to
\hat{P}^{(k-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}})
.
Thus
(G11)
holds.

Note that
(P12)
and the definition of
\hat{A}_{k}
imply that

G^{(k)}_{1}\triangle H^{(k)}_{1}\subseteq\binom{V}{k}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k}(\mathscr{Q}_{1}^{(1)}).

Since
\mathscr{Q}_{1}
is
(1/a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}},\varepsilon,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
-equitable and
1/a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}}\ll\nu,1/k
, we obtain

\left|\binom{V}{k}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{k}(\mathscr{Q}_{1}^{(1)})\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: eta a1}}}{{\leq}}\frac{k^{2}}{a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}}}\binom{n}{k}\leq\nu\binom{n}{k}.

This proves
(G12)
.
∎

Step 2
.

Refining
\mathscr{Q}_{2}
into
\mathscr{P}_{2}
which mirrors
\mathscr{P}_{1}
.

We have now set up the required definitions for the objects on

.
By using Lemma
5.10
with

,

,

(1/a_{1}^{\mathscr{Q}},\varepsilon^{1/3},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
-equitable family of partitions

.
By considering an appropriate
\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}
-labelling,

for each

,
(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b^{\prime})\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a_{j}^{\mathscr{P}}]

P^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b^{\prime})\subseteq Q^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b)\text{ if and only if }(\hat{\mathbf{y}},b^{\prime})\in A_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},b).

Again Lemma
5.4
and the fact that
\varepsilon^{\prime}\ll\nu,\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}\|_{\infty}^{-k}

imply that for each
j\in[k-1]
,

,
we have

(7.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))|&=(1\pm\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}m^{j+1}\kern 5.0pt\text{ and }\\
|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{Q}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{x}}))|&=(1\pm\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}m^{j+1}.\end{split}

Let

G^{(k)}_{2}:=H^{(k)}_{2}\cap\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}_{k}}\mathcal{K}_{k}(\hat{P}_{2}^{(k-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}})).

Similarly as in Claim
1
,
we conclude the following.

\mathscr{P}_{2}
is an
(\varepsilon^{\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}},k})
-equitable partition of
G_{2}^{(k)}
.

(G22)

|G^{(k)}_{2}\triangle H^{(k)}_{2}|\leq\nu\binom{n}{k}
.

Step 3
.

Modifying
\mathscr{O}_{1}
into

, respectively, and obtain

(O
′
11)

\mathscr{P}_{1}\prec\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{1}
.

(O
′
12)

\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{1}
is a

(O
′
13)

, we have

\varepsilon_{0}+\nu^{1/20}\leq\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}}\|_{\infty}^{-4^{k}}\cdot\varepsilon_{\ref{lem: counting}}(\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}}\|_{\infty}^{-1},\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}}\|_{\infty}^{-1},k-1,k).

Thus Lemma
5.4
implies for any
j\in[k-1]
and

, we have

(7.5)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))|\geq\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}n^{j+1}.

Also,
(O′12)
implies that
for all
j\in[k-2]
,

and

(7.6)

\displaystyle|O^{\prime(j+1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)|\geq(1/a_{j+1}^{\mathscr{O}}-2\varepsilon_{0})|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))|\geq\varepsilon_{0}^{2/3}n^{j+1}.

Moreover, by (O
′

′

, we can apply Lemma

.

Step 4
.

Describing
\mathscr{O}_{1}^{\prime}
in terms of its refinement
\mathscr{P}_{1}
.

We now describe how the partition classes and polyads of
\mathscr{O}_{1}^{\prime}
can be expressed in terms of
\mathscr{P}_{1}
.
This description will be used to construct

from

in Step
5
.

For each
j\in[k-2]
,
our next aim is to define
B_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)
for
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
and
b\in[a^{\mathscr{O}}_{j+1}]

in a similar way as we defined
A_{j+1}(\mathbf{x},b)
for
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{Q}})
and
b\in[a^{\mathscr{Q}}_{j+1}]
in (
7.1
).
To this end, for each
b\in[a_{1}^{\mathscr{O}}]
, let

B_{1}(b,b):=\{(b^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\hat{A}(1,0,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{1}]:P^{(1)}_{1}(b^{\prime},b^{\prime})\subseteq O^{\prime(1)}_{1}(b,b)\}.

For each
j\in[k-1]
, let

\hat{B}_{j+1}:=\left\{\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}):\left|\mathbf{u}^{(1)}_{*}\cap\{b^{\prime}:(b^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in B_{1}(b,b)\}\right|\leq 1\text{ for each }b\in[a_{1}^{\mathscr{O}}]\right\}.

Note that this easily implies that

(7.8)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}\kern 5.0pt\text{if and only if}\kern 5.0pt\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\mathscr{O}_{1}^{\prime(1)}).

Consider any
j\in[k-1]
and
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
.
Let

(7.9)

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}):=\left\{\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}):\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O^{\prime}}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right\}.

Together with (O
′
11) and Proposition
2.7
(xi) this implies that

(7.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P_{1}}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})).

Moreover, if
j\in[k-2]
and
b\in[a_{j+1}^{\mathscr{O}}]
, let

(7.11)

\displaystyle B_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b):=\left\{(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a^{\mathscr{P}}_{j+1}]:P^{(j+1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\subseteq O^{\prime(j+1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\right\}.

Thus
(O′11)
, (
7.2
) and (
7.5
) imply that for all
j\in[k-1]
and
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})

(7.12)

\displaystyle|\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})|\geq\frac{|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O^{\prime}}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))|}{(1+\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}n^{j+1}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{1/2}\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{\binom{j+1}{i}}.

Similarly,

(O′11)
, (
7.3
) and (
7.6
)
imply that for all
j\in[k-1]\setminus\{1\}
,
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
and
b\in[a_{j}^{\mathscr{O}}]
,

(7.13)

\displaystyle|B_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)|\geq\frac{|O^{\prime(j-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)|}{(1+2\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j}{i}}n^{j}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{2/3}\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{\binom{j}{i}}>0.

Note that by Proposition
2.7
(xi) and
(O′11)
, for each
j\in[k-1]
, we have

(7.14)

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})):\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\}\prec\{\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O^{\prime}}_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})):\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})\}.

Together with (
7.8
) and Proposition
2.7
(vi) applied to
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{1}
, this implies that
\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}
if and only if
\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
for some
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
.
Thus for each
j\in[k-1]
,

(7.15)

\{\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}):\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})\}
forms a partition of
\hat{B}_{j+1}
.

The following observations relate polyads and partition classes of
\mathscr{O}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathscr{P}_{1}
.
They will be used in the proof of Claim
3
to relate
\mathscr{O}_{2}^{\prime}
(which is constructed in Step
5
) and
\mathscr{P}_{2}
.

Claim 2
.

(i)

For all
j\in[k-1]\setminus\{1\}
and
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
, we have

\displaystyle\bigcup_{b\in[a_{j}^{\mathscr{O}}]}B_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)=\hat{B}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})\times[a_{j}^{\mathscr{P}}].

(ii)

For all
j\in[k-1]
and
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
, we have

\left\{(\hat{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{u}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{*}}):\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}),\hat{\mathbf{v}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{u}}\right\}\subseteq\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{z}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{w}}}B_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{z}},\mathbf{w}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{z}^{(1)}_{*}}).

Proof.

We first prove (i).
Note that for all
j\in[k-1]\setminus\{1\}
,
(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})\times[a_{j}^{\mathscr{P}}]
and

(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\in\hat{A}(j,j-1,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})\times[a_{j}^{\mathscr{O}}]
with
(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\in B_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)
, we have

P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\subseteq O^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})).

Since
P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{P}^{(j-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))
, this means

\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{P}^{(j-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))\cap\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{O^{\prime}}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\neq\emptyset.

By (
7.14
) this in turn implies that

\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{P}^{(j-1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j}(\hat{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j-1)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))
, and thus
\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
.
On the other hand, if
\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
, then
(O′11)
implies that for each
b^{\prime}\in[a_{j}^{\mathscr{P}}]
there exists
b\in[a_{j}^{\mathscr{O}}]
such that
P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\subseteq O^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)
, and thus
(\hat{\mathbf{u}},b^{\prime})\in B_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)
.

We now prove (ii).
Recall that for each
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
,
\hat{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
satisfies (
2.12
).
Together with (O
′
11) this implies that

(7.16)

\displaystyle\hat{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{z}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{w}}}{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{z}},\mathbf{w}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{z}^{(1)}_{*}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: B hat bw b def}}}{{=}}\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{z}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{w}}}\bigcup_{(\hat{\mathbf{v}},b^{\prime})\in B_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{z}},\mathbf{w}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{z}^{(1)}_{*}})}P^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{v}},b^{\prime}).

Then

\displaystyle(\hat{\mathbf{v}},b^{\prime})\in\{(\hat{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{u}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{*}}):\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}),\hat{\mathbf{v}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{u}}\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:hatPconsistsofP(x,b)},\eqref{eq: B hat bw b def2}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}

\displaystyle\exists\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}}):\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O^{\prime}}_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})),P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{v}},b^{\prime})\subseteq\hat{P}_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:polyad}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}

\displaystyle\exists(I,J)\in P_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{v}},b^{\prime})\times\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O^{\prime}}_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})):I\subseteq J

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:polyad},({\rm O}^{\prime}11)}}{{\Longrightarrow}}

\displaystyle P^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{v}},b^{\prime})\subseteq\hat{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Phat1}}}{{\Longrightarrow}}

\displaystyle(\hat{\mathbf{v}},b^{\prime})\in\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{z}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{w}}}B_{j}(\hat{\mathbf{z}},\mathbf{w}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{z}^{(1)}_{*}}).

This proves the claim.
∎

Claim 3
.

\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{2}
is a family of partitions on

. Moreover,

Proof.

We will prove Claim

by applying the criteria in Lemma
4.2
.
For each

,

(OP1)

(OP2)
ℓ

\displaystyle\hat{O}_{*}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})={\hat{O}_{2}}^{\prime(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}).

It is easy to check that (OP1)
1
–(OP2)
1

Claim

Subclaim 1
.

For each
j\in[\ell]
and

Proof.

Consider
j\in[\ell]
and
\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})
. Note that

\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{u}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:hatPconsistsofP(x,b)}}}{{=}}\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{v}}\leq_{j,j-1}\hat{\mathbf{u}}}P_{2}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{u}^{(j)}_{\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{*}}).

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}}\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: hat Bj if crossing in O2}}}{{=}}\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\mathscr{O}_{2}^{\prime(1)})=\bigcup_{\hat{\mathbf{w}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}})}\mathcal{K}_{j+1}({\hat{O}_{2}}^{\prime(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})).

(j+1)

We claim that

.
Indeed if not, then by (

), there exists

.
The fact that this holds for arbitrary

Claim 4
.

For all
j\in[k-1]
,

,

-graph

(7.29)

2

(7.33)

if

Subclaim 2
.

For all
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})

, we have that

(\varepsilon^{\prime 1/2},d(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))

Proof.

hypergraphs,

Thus the union lemma (Lemma

If

.
If

.
∎

In order to show that
O_{2}^{\prime(j+1)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)
is
(\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20})
-regular with respect to
\hat{O}_{2}^{\prime(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
,
we will transfer all the calculations about hypergraph densities from the hypergraphs on
V_{2}

to the hypergraphs on
V_{1}
,
because there we have much better control over their structure.
To this end we first use Lemma
6.2
to show the existence of two hypergraphs
J_{1}^{(j)},J_{2}^{(j)}
on
V_{1},V_{2}
, respectively,
that exhibit a very similar structure in terms of their densities and where

is very close to

.
Consequently,
J_{1}^{(j)}
also resembles
F^{(j)}
.

More precisely, we now apply Lemma
6.2
with
F^{(j)},\{\mathscr{P}_{2}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{j},\{\mathscr{P}_{1}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{j},j
playing the roles of
H^{(k-1)},\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q},k-1
respectively (we can do this by
(P11)
,
(G21)
and Claim
3
).
We obtain
j
-graphs
J_{2}^{(j)}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j}(\mathscr{P}_{2}^{(1)})
on
V_{2}
and
J_{1}^{(j)}\subseteq\mathcal{K}_{j}(\mathscr{P}_{1}^{(1)})
on
V_{1}
such that

(J1)

|J_{2}^{(j)}\triangle F^{(j)}|\leq\nu\binom{m}{j}
, and

(J2)

d(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))=d(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\pm\nu

for each
\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})
.

Our next aim is to estimate
|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})|
in terms of
|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})|
.

(7.34)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\right|\kern 5.0pt=\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: 2 polyad size}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(1\pm\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}m^{j+1}\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}d(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm\ref{item:J2},\eqref{eq: 1 polyad size}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(1\pm 3\nu)\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}(d(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\pm\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(1\pm 3\nu)\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left(\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}\hskip-17.07182pt\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\pm\nu\hskip-17.07182pt\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}\hskip-17.07182pt\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}(|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})|\pm 5\nu n^{j+1}).

Similarly, we obtain

(7.35)

\displaystyle\hskip-62.59596pt\left|O^{\prime(j+1)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\cap\kern 5.0pt\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\right|=\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}\hskip-8.5359pt\left|O^{\prime(j+1)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}_{2}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm Subcl.~\ref{claim:O'regular}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}\left(d(\hat{\mathbf{y}})\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\pm\varepsilon^{\prime 1/4}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: 2 polyad size},\eqref{eq: when d hat by 0}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\left((1\pm\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}m^{j+1}\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\hskip-8.5359ptd(\hat{\mathbf{y}})d(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right)\pm\varepsilon^{\prime 1/4}m^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm\ref{item:J2}},\eqref{eq: 1 polyad size}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\left((1\pm 3\nu)\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\hskip-8.5359ptd(\hat{\mathbf{y}})d(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right)\pm 4\nu m^{j+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(1\pm 3\nu)\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left(\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\hskip-8.5359ptd(\hat{\mathbf{y}})\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\right)\pm 4\nu m^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: when d hat by 0},\rm Subcl.~\ref{claim:O'regular}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(1\pm 3\nu)\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left(\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\hskip 14.22636pt\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\hat{A}(j+1,j,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{P}})}\hskip-25.6073pt\left|O^{\prime(j+1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}_{1}^{(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|\right)\pm 5\nu m^{j+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left(\left|O^{\prime(j+1)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\right|\pm 10\nu n^{j+1}\right).

Note that
(J1)
implies that

(7.36)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)}))\triangle\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(F^{(j)})\right|\leq\nu\binom{m}{j}\cdot m\leq\nu m^{j+1}

Since
F^{(j)}\subseteq\hat{O^{\prime}}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
by assumption, (
7.36
) implies that

(7.37)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\setminus\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right|\leq\nu m^{j+1}.

We can transfer (
7.37
) to the corresponding graphs on
V_{1}
as follows:

(7.38)

\displaystyle\hskip-79.6678pt\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\setminus\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: B hat bw b def2}}}{{=}}\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\notin\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: 1 polyad size}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle(1+\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}n^{j+1}\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\notin\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\hskip-8.5359ptd(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm\ref{item:J2}}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\left((1+\nu)\prod_{i=1}^{j}(a^{\mathscr{P}}_{i})^{-\binom{j+1}{i}}n^{j+1}\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\hat{\mathbf{y}}\notin\hat{B}_{j+1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})}\hskip-8.5359ptd(\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\mid\hat{P}^{(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{y}}))\right)+2\nu n^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: 2 polyad size},\eqref{eq: cKj+1 hat O'2(j) is..}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\frac{n^{j+1}}{m^{j+1}}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\setminus\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right|+5\nu n^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: J2 hat O' bw}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle 6\nu n^{j+1}.

Next we show that
|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))|
is not too small:

(7.39)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O^{\prime}}^{(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: J1 setminus O'}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\right|-6\nu n^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: J2 J1}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\frac{n^{j+1}}{m^{j+1}}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\right|-11\nu n^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: cKj J2 triangle F}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\frac{n^{j+1}}{m^{j+1}}\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(F^{(j)})\right|-12\nu n^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: F j-1 big}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\frac{n^{j+1}}{m^{j+1}}(\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20})\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right|-12\nu n^{j+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: Bj hat bw size},\eqref{eq: cK hat O' size}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle(\varepsilon_{0}+2\nu^{1/20})\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right|.

Recall that
d
was defined in (
7.1
). We now can combine our estimates to conclude that

(7.40)

\displaystyle\hskip-82.51282pt\left|O^{\prime(j+1)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(F^{(j)})\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: O2 cap J2},\eqref{eq: cKj J2 triangle F}}}{{=}}\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left(\left|{O^{\prime}_{1}}^{(j+1)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\right|\pm 20\nu n^{j+1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: O'1 is also good partition too},\eqref{eq: cK J1 O' hat bw big},\text{\ref{item:O'12}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\left((d\pm(\varepsilon_{0}+2\nu^{1/20}))\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\cap\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}_{1}^{\prime(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))\right|\pm 20\nu n^{j+1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt\frac{m^{j+1}}{n^{j+1}}\hskip-1.42271pt(d\pm(\varepsilon_{0}\hskip-1.42271pt+\hskip-1.42271pt2\nu^{1/20}))\hskip-1.42271pt\left(\hskip-1.42271pt|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})|\hskip-1.42271pt-\hskip-1.42271pt|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{1}^{(j)})\hskip-1.42271pt\setminus\hskip-1.42271pt\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{1}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}))|\hskip-1.42271pt\right)\hskip-1.42271pt\pm\hskip-1.42271pt20\nu m^{j+1}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: J2 J1},\eqref{eq: J1 setminus O'}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(d\pm(\varepsilon_{0}+2\nu^{1/20}))\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(J_{2}^{(j)})\right|\pm 40\nu m^{j+1}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: cKj J2 triangle F}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(d\pm(\varepsilon_{0}+2\nu^{1/20}))\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(F^{(j)})\right|\pm 50\nu m^{j+1}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=

\displaystyle\hskip-8.5359pt(d\pm(\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20}))\left|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(F^{(j)})\right|.

Here, we obtain the final inequality since (
7.12
), (
7.1
) and (
7.29
) imply

|\mathcal{K}_{j+1}(F^{(j)})|\geq\varepsilon_{0}^{2}m^{j+1}
and
\nu\ll\varepsilon_{0}
.
(
7.40
) holds for all
F^{(j)}\subseteq\hat{O}_{2}^{\prime(j)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
satisfying (
7.29
), thus
O_{2}^{\prime(j+1)}(\hat{\mathbf{w}},b)
is
(\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20},d)
-regular with respect to
\hat{O}^{\prime(j)}_{2}(\hat{\mathbf{w}})
.
This with the definition of
d
completes the proof of Claim
4
.
∎

Claim
4
and (
7.19
) show that
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{2}
is a
(1/a_{1}^{\mathscr{O}},\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},2\nu^{1/20})
-equitable family of partitions
which is also an
(\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})
-partition of
G_{2}^{(k)}
(as defined in Section
2.4.2
).
Note that
(\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20})/3\leq\varepsilon_{0}/2
, thus
((\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20})/3,\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})
is a regularity instance.
Since
|G_{2}^{(k)}\triangle H_{2}^{(k)}|\leq\nu\binom{m}{k}
by
(G22)
, this means that
we can apply Lemma
5.11
with the following objects and parameters.

object/parameter

\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{2}

\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{2}

\nu

\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/20}

d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k}

G^{(k)}_{2}

H^{(k)}_{2}

playing the role of

\mathscr{P}

\mathscr{Q}

\nu

\varepsilon

d_{\mathbf{a},k}

H^{(k)}

G^{(k)}

Hence
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{2}
is also an
(\varepsilon_{0}+4\nu^{1/20},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})
-partition of
H_{2}^{(k)}
.

Step 6
.

Adjusting
\mathscr{O}_{2}^{\prime}
into an equipartition
\mathscr{O}_{2}
.

Finally,
we modify
\mathscr{O}_{2}^{\prime}
to turn it from an ‘almost’ equipartition into an equipartition
\mathscr{O}_{2}
.
For this we apply Lemma
5.12
with
\mathscr{O}^{\prime}_{2},H_{2}^{(k)}\varepsilon_{0}+4\nu^{1/20},2\nu^{1/20},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k}
playing the roles of

respectively.
This guarantees an
(\varepsilon_{0}+3\nu^{1/200},\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},d_{\mathbf{a}^{\mathscr{O}},k})

\mathscr{O}_{2}
of
